Examining Retro and Kitsch in AI Art Prompts
The sources and conversation history provide numerous examples of how retro and kitsch aesthetics are incorporated into AI-generated artwork, often as integral components of a larger stylistic and conceptual framework.

Retro Aesthetics
Nostalgic Technologies and Styles: The sources emphasize a strong presence of retro-futuristic and nostalgic elements in the prompts, referencing technologies and styles from specific past eras.
Examples include "1978 Corolla wagon with temporal-displacement wraparound-glyptodon engine" [1] and "Old Piedmontese hand-worked Volkswagen bus" with ornate owl-based designs [2, 3].
These prompts juxtapose familiar, outdated technologies (cars, buses) with fantastical or futuristic concepts, creating a sense of temporal dissonance and playful absurdity.
Midcentury Design and Kitsch Terminology: The conversation history explores the use of Midcentury design principles and kitsch terminology in prompts, focusing on clean lines, retro materials, and nostalgic tones [4-9].
Examples include a hyper-stylized kitchenette constructed from brushed aluminum, lacquered plywood, and frosted Plexiglas [4] and a lounge chair infused with Eames-era modernism and 1960s tiki bar kitsch [5].
These prompts combine the sleekness of Midcentury modernism with the playful, often ironic, use of kitsch elements to create a retro-futuristic aesthetic that is both familiar and subversive.
Kitsch Aesthetics
Exaggeration and Irony: The sources highlight the use of kitsch as an aesthetic tool to exaggerate nostalgic tropes and push them to absurd extremes [10].
This often involves the appropriation of lowbrow cultural references and the embrace of "guilt-free bad taste" to create a sense of subversive enjoyment.
Specific examples of kitsch elements include references to Liberace, flapper valkyries, and He-Man figures in unlikely contexts.
Blending Kitsch with High Culture: The sources demonstrate a tendency to blend kitsch aesthetics with high culture or intellectual concepts, creating a jarring yet humorous juxtaposition.
For instance, the prompt featuring John Waters as Gandhi [10] places a filmmaker known for his campy and transgressive aesthetic in the role of a revered figure of peace and spirituality.
This clash of high and low culture contributes to the overall absurdity and satirical nature of the prompts.
The Role of Retro and Kitsch
The incorporation of retro and kitsch elements in AI art prompts serves several purposes:

Evoke Nostalgia and Familiarity: By referencing familiar objects, styles, and cultural icons from the past, the prompts create a sense of nostalgia and connect with the viewer's personal experiences and memories.
Amplify Absurdity and Humor: The juxtaposition of retro and kitsch elements with futuristic, surreal, or incongruous concepts heightens the absurdity and generates humor through unexpected contrasts and playful recontextualization.
Critique Consumerism and Cultural Trends: The use of kitsch and nostalgic imagery often serves as a commentary on consumer culture, the commodification of history, and the cyclical nature of trends.
For example, the "Warlord-astronaut, now sponsored by HyperTech" prompt [11] satirizes both the glorification of warriors throughout history and the influence of modern influencer marketing.
Conclusion
Retro and kitsch aesthetics are not merely superficial elements in AI art prompts; they are integral to the overall stylistic and conceptual framework. By blending nostalgic imagery with futuristic concepts and juxtaposing high and low culture, these prompts create a complex and often satirical commentary on history, culture, and contemporary society.

Sources of Art and Craft Terminology in AI Art Prompts
The sources showcase a fascinating array of art and craft terminology used in AI art prompts, often blended in unconventional and humorous ways. This terminology draws from diverse sources, creating a complex and layered aesthetic vocabulary.

1. Historical Art Movements and Styles:

Prompts frequently reference well-known art movements like Art Deco, Art Nouveau, Baroque, Rococo, and Surrealism [1-4].
These references often appear alongside anachronistic elements, creating unexpected juxtapositions, such as a "steampunk astronaut with baroque rococo details" [2].
More obscure movements like linea clara, jagerkunst, and sino-moche also appear, suggesting a broader knowledge of art history [5-7].
2. Cultural and Historical References:

Prompts draw from diverse cultural and historical periods, including ancient Egyptian, Sumerian, Victorian, Aztec, and cyberpunk aesthetics [2, 4, 5, 8-10].
Specific cultures like Jurchen and Pennsylvania Dutch are referenced, indicating an interest in exploring lesser-known traditions [11, 12].
These cultural elements are often combined in unexpected ways, such as "hyper-Aztec industrial complexes" or "chrono-victorian fusion panels" [13].
3. Material Science and Technology:

Prompts frequently employ terminology from material science and technology, including "aerogel, iridescent ceramic, spun glass, mycelium, rare earth oxides, volcanic ash, and photonic crystals" [14].
Advanced technologies like holography, nanotechnology, and cryogenics appear alongside more traditional materials like wood, lace, and glass [13, 15, 16].
This blending of materials creates a sense of technological anachronism and surreal materiality, such as "steampunk lace circuitry" or "Victorian terrarium lamps" [13].
4. Photography and Cinematic Techniques:

Prompts often specify photographic and cinematic techniques, including camera models, film types, lenses, and lighting styles [4, 17].
References to "Kodachrome," "Ektarchrome," "Phase One Imago," and specific lenses suggest a familiarity with photographic history and equipment [5, 8, 18].
Imaginary or unusual photographic techniques like "ambiant-microwave aquascopic radial gopro telescopy" also appear [19, 20].
5. Neologisms and Creative Wordplay:

The sources highlight a distinct tendency to create neologisms and engage in creative wordplay [21-23].
Prefixes like "hyper-," "ultra-," "retro-," and "chrono-" are combined with existing words to form new concepts like "hypermaximalist" or "retrotemporal" [24, 25].
Surreal and humorous combinations like "hydrodynamosquatchmeter" and "Canon bacon-camera microwave photography" illustrate the playful use of language [26, 27].
6. Appropriation of Marketing and Technical Jargon:

The sources observe the use of quasi-marketing language and technical jargon to create a sense of artificial luxury or authority [28-30].
Prompts like "Opulent luxury deluxe imperial magisterial coal-fired kinetic old Bavarian jagerkunst" and "luxury hypodermic cryobot" utilize an overabundance of descriptors and technical terms [31, 32].
This appropriation of language further contributes to the ironic and often satirical tone of the prompts.
Conclusion:

The sources illustrate a rich and eclectic approach to art and craft terminology in AI art prompts. By drawing from diverse sources and employing creative wordplay, these prompts generate a unique aesthetic vocabulary that blends the historical, the technological, and the absurd. This approach highlights the playful and experimental nature of AI art prompting and its potential to challenge traditional notions of artistic language and expression.

Examining Contradictions within AI Art Prompting Styles
The sources and our conversation history reveal a dynamic interplay between opposing approaches within the realm of AI art prompting, particularly with Midjourney. While a maximalist aesthetic, characterized by extensive detail and layered juxtapositions, dominates the landscape, various exceptions and contradictory techniques challenge this prevailing trend.

Contrasting Minimalism and Intentional Realism
One striking contradiction lies in the tension between minimalism and maximalism. Despite the abundance of hyper-detailed prompts, a desire for restraint and simplicity also surfaces. Source [1] mentions specific Midjourney tools, like personalization codes and style weights, that offer nuanced control over the outputs, potentially leading to streamlined visuals.

Furthermore, our conversation history highlights a user's preference for "monochromatic, metallic, and solid-tone descriptors" to achieve a more "grounded, coherent style," suggesting that some users within this movement seek a minimalist aesthetic [1]. This preference for visual clarity contrasts with the tendency towards excessive detail found in many prompts.

In addition to minimalist preferences, there's a noticeable emphasis on photorealism in some instances [2]. This pursuit of realism clashes with the more prevalent trend of generating surreal and impossible scenarios [2]. This suggests a potential sub-category within the movement where AI is used to enhance photographic aesthetics rather than solely create outlandish imagery [2].

Humor and Self-Awareness Undermining Maximalism
Another layer of contradiction emerges from the use of humor and self-awareness to subvert the very maximalism often celebrated. The inclusion of platitudes like "beautiful" and "wonderful" within absurd contexts generates a jarring contrast, highlighting the potential emptiness or superficiality of excessive detail.

The tendency to coin nonsensical or ironic names for the movement further demonstrates this self-aware critique of overblown aesthetics. Terms like "McLuxoiserie" or "Spectroglam-oiserie," mentioned in our conversation, exemplify this tendency to satirize the movement's own proclivity for excess. This playful undermining of maximalism adds a layer of irony and complexity to the movement's overall aesthetic and philosophy.

Individual Preferences and the Role of Chance
Further complicating matters is the recognition of individual variations and the role of chance in shaping outcomes. Our conversation highlights a user's evolving preferences, transitioning from "amorphous descriptors" like jellyfish and coral to more structured forms [3]. This demonstrates the fluidity of individual tastes and how they influence the direction of this evolving movement.

Source [4] acknowledges the element of serendipity in Midjourney. It states, "sometimes you just need to try several times before midjourney accidentally works," and suggests using "chaos" settings to introduce randomness. This reliance on chance contradicts the notion of absolute control implied by the hyper-specific prompts. It underscores the fact that even with meticulous attempts to control the output, unpredictability remains inherent in AI image generation.

Conclusion
These contradictions and exceptions suggest that the AI art movement is not a monolithic entity with a single aesthetic agenda. Instead, it's a dynamic and evolving landscape where conflicting approaches coexist and even enrich each other. The interplay between maximalism and minimalism, photorealism and surrealism, humor and seriousness, and control and chance creates a complex and fascinating tapestry of creative possibilities. Examining these contradictions offers a deeper understanding of the multifaceted relationship between human creativity and the evolving capabilities of AI art.

Exceptions and Contradictions: Challenging the Maximalist Aesthetic
While the sources heavily feature maximalist aesthetics, characterized by dense layering, intricate details, and a profusion of elements, several exceptions and counterpoints emerge, complicating our understanding of the overall style.

Minimalism and Reduction: Despite the prevalence of hyper-detailed prompts, there are instances where minimalism and reduction take center stage. Source [1] describes personalization codes and style weights, suggesting a desire for nuanced control that can lead to simplified outputs. Further, our conversation history notes a user preference for "monochromatic, metallic, and solid-tone descriptors" to achieve a "grounded, coherent style" [2, 3]. These preferences contrast with the prevailing maximalism, hinting at a desire for restraint and focused aesthetics within this broader movement.

Emphasis on Realism: The sources occasionally prioritize photorealism over fantastical or hyper-stylized imagery. Source [4] explicitly states, "realism seems to be a combination of factors... photographic styles." This emphasis on capturing reality clashes with the dominant trend of creating surreal and impossible scenarios. It suggests a potential sub-category within the movement that seeks to leverage AI for its ability to mimic and enhance photographic aesthetics, rather than solely focusing on the creation of the outlandish.

Humorous Undermining of Maximalism: The sources also contain elements that humorously undermine the very maximalism they often embrace. The use of platitudes like "beautiful" and "wonderful" in otherwise absurd contexts [5-8] creates a jarring contrast, highlighting the potential emptiness or superficiality of excessive detail. Similarly, the tendency to coin nonsensical or ironic names for the movement, such as "McLuxoiserie" or "Spectroglam-oiserie" [9, 10], reflects a self-aware critique of overblown aesthetics.

Our conversation history highlights a user's evolving preferences, moving from "amorphous descriptors" like jellyfish and coral to more structured forms [11, 12]. These shifts in taste and emphasis demonstrate that individual users within this broader aesthetic movement are not bound by a single rigid set of rules. They are constantly experimenting, pushing boundaries, and challenging the conventions they themselves help establish.

The Role of Chance: Source [4] acknowledges the element of chance in Midjourney, noting that sometimes "you just need to try several times before midjourney accidentally works" and suggesting the use of "chaos" settings to introduce randomness. This reliance on serendipity contradicts the notion of complete control often implied by the hyper-specific prompting style. It suggests that, despite attempts to meticulously dictate the output, unexpected and unpredictable results are an inherent part of the process.

The Vast Landscape of AI Art Prompting Styles
The sources collectively reveal a remarkable range and breadth of prompting styles employed in AI art generation, particularly with Midjourney. These styles are far from monolithic, demonstrating an evolving landscape of creative exploration and linguistic experimentation.

Hyper-Specificity and Layered Detail: A dominant trend across the sources is the use of hyper-specific language, densely packed with adjectives, materials, techniques, and references [1-3]. This creates a sense of overwhelming detail, almost as if the prompter is attempting to control every aspect of the final image. Prompts read like lists of attributes rather than coherent sentences [4]. This style prioritizes visual and material qualities over narrative, resulting in a rich tapestry of descriptive elements [5]. Examples include meticulously listing camera specifications, lighting setups, film types, and even imaginary image processing methods [6, 7].

Juxtaposition and Conceptual Dissonance: The sources frequently highlight the use of juxtaposition and conceptual dissonance to generate surprising and often humorous results [8-10]. Prompts combine elements from drastically different time periods, cultures, genres, or technological states, forcing the AI to reconcile these incongruities [11, 12]. This strategy creates a sense of controlled chaos, leading to images that are both visually arresting and conceptually challenging. Examples include "footage from the Cassini-Jukebox mission to a Romani caravan on Jupiter" [9, 11], "Adobe multi-storey baroque Mennonite-goth onionskin Tsarist split-screen acid bomb" [13], and "Old timey maritime sailors bar with an aquarium and Precision-engineered mauvine Opulent spherical 1970s futuristic kitchenette apparition tube" [14].

Quasi-Marketing Jargon and Unusual Registers: A distinct and recurring style involves employing quasi-marketing jargon and unusual registers [15, 16]. This involves appropriating the language of advertising, product descriptions, and corporate branding to describe bizarre or impossible scenarios. The result is an ironic and often humorous clash between the slick, polished language of consumerism and the inherent absurdity of the imagined worlds [17, 18]. Examples include phrases like "Opulent luxury deluxe imperial magisterial coal-fired kinetic old Bavarian jägerkunst" [19] and "Luxury frostpunk buddhagirl levitating in o'neill cylinder colony volcano blast-forest" [20].

Nostalgia, Retrofuturism, and Kitsch: The sources also show a strong affinity for nostalgia, retrofuturism, and kitsch [21, 22]. This manifests in prompts that draw heavily from past visions of the future, vintage technology, and sentimental imagery. Examples include "1989 promotional photo of Paula Abdul with her temporal-displacement Glyptodon backup dancers" [23] and "1980s television screen still with retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy." [24] The blending of nostalgic elements with futuristic or surreal concepts creates a disorienting yet captivating aesthetic that plays on our collective cultural memory while simultaneously challenging our understanding of the past and future.

Technical Exploration and Artistic References: Many prompts incorporate detailed technical language and artistic references, demonstrating a high level of familiarity with image generation tools, photographic techniques, and art history [14, 25]. This includes specifying camera models, film types, resolutions, lighting setups, and even referencing specific artists or art movements [26-29]. While these details may not always translate directly into the generated images, they contribute to a sense of artificial realism and informed creativity.

Iterative Refinement and Experimentation: The sources emphasize the iterative nature of prompt creation, with users constantly tweaking parameters, combining elements, and exploring new approaches to achieve increasingly specific or unexpected results [3, 30]. This experimentation is reflected in the use of Midjourney's advanced features, such as style weights, character references, and versioning [30, 31]. This ongoing refinement underscores the dynamic relationship between human creativity and AI capabilities.

Beyond Categorization:

While it is possible to identify recurring styles and techniques, the sources also suggest that the full range of prompting styles is still emerging. Many prompts defy easy categorization, employing unique combinations of language, imagery, and concepts.

This ongoing evolution is exemplified in your efforts to coin a name for this aesthetic movement, brainstorming terms like "Hyperstitional Absurdism," "Mockterialism," "Tweakeroque," and "Reinturpentine" [32-34].

The inherent difficulty in defining this style underscores its experimental and boundary-pushing nature. It is a style in flux, constantly redefining itself through the interplay of human creativity and the evolving capabilities of AI image generation technology.

By incorporating these materials and techniques, the prompts tap into our cultural fascination with the past, reminding us of the beauty and craftsmanship of older objects and prompting the AI to generate imagery that evokes a sense of history and nostalgia.

Fabricating the Fantastic: Neologisms and Unusual Word Formation in Midjourney Prompts
The sources, comprised of conversations analyzing a collection of Midjourney prompts, showcase a fascinating tendency to use neologisms (newly coined words or expressions) and unusual word formations to create absurd and fantastical imagery. This linguistic playfulness is a key characteristic of the prompts, contributing to their surreal and humorous aesthetic.

1. Prefix Piling and Affix Stacking
The prompts exhibit a penchant for stacking prefixes and suffixes to create exaggerated and often nonsensical terms. This technique amplifies existing meanings, pushing them to their extremes and often resulting in humorous hyperbole:

Hyper-: "Hyperbaric," "hyperopulent," "hyperornate," "hypermaximalist," "hyperminimalist," "hypercomplex," "hyperfractal" [1]

Chrono-: "Chrono-dynamic" [1, 2]
Non-: "Non-newtonian," "non-euclidean" [1]
These prefixes, typically used to intensify or modify existing words, are piled onto one another to create words like "fractal-ultrafluffy" or "recursively-tufted," pushing the boundaries of language and generating a sense of exaggerated visual density [3, 4].

2. Blending and Hybridization
The prompts frequently blend existing words or concepts to create new terms that evoke hybrid imagery. This technique allows for the fusion of seemingly disparate elements, resulting in surprising and often nonsensical visual combinations:

Mandelbulb: A fusion of "Mandelbrot" (referring to the Mandelbrot set, a mathematical fractal) and "bulb," suggesting a visually complex and bulbous form [3, 4].

These blended terms challenge conventional categorization, forcing the AI to reconcile contrasting visual qualities and generating unexpected outcomes.

3. Borrowing and Adapting
The prompts borrow terms from various fields, including science, technology, and art, to create a sense of pseudo-expertise and technical precision. These borrowed terms are often applied to absurd or fantastical contexts, heightening the comedic effect:

This strategy creates a clash between the serious and the absurd, mimicking the language of specialists while describing concepts that defy logical explanation.

4. Playful Invention and Subversion
The prompts often feature playfully invented words or phrases that are intentionally nonsensical or have no clear meaning. This linguistic inventiveness contributes to the overall absurdity and humor of the prompts:

"Angora nuclear core meltdown": A nonsensical yet evocative phrase that combines the soft texture of angora wool with the catastrophic imagery of a nuclear meltdown [5, 6].

The prompts utilize various recurring constructions and permutations to create a sense of materiality and layered complexity:

-Clad: Used to describe objects covered in a specific material, such as "Jupiter-clad" [5].

Glossary of Key Terms
Prompt Engineering: The process of crafting effective and specific text prompts for AI image generators to guide the desired output.
Aspect Ratio (–-ar): The proportional relationship between the width and height of an image, expressed as a ratio (e.g., 16:9).
Chaos Parameter (–-chaos): A setting in AI image generators that introduces a degree of randomness or unpredictability into the generation process.
Prompt Weights: Numerical values assigned to terms within a prompt to control their relative importance in influencing the generated image.
Chinoiserie: A European art style inspired by East Asian artistic traditions, often characterized by intricate patterns, exotic imagery, and lacquered finishes.
Biopunk: A science fiction subgenre that explores the intersection of biotechnology and punk culture, typically featuring themes of body modification, genetic engineering, and societal rebellion.
Midjourney: A popular AI image generation platform that allows users to create images from text prompts using machine learning algorithms.
AI Art Generation: The use of artificial intelligence algorithms to create visual artwork.
Hypermaximalism: An aesthetic characterized by an overwhelming abundance of detail, ornamentation, and sensory stimulation.

AI Image Generation and Prompt Engineering FAQ
1. What are some common prefixes and suffixes used in AI image generation prompts?
Prefixes: hyper-, ultra-, retro-, chrono-, non-, antediluvian-, psychotropic-, external-, wraparound-, recursive-, multi-, bicolor-, temporal-, supernova-, solar-, cryogenic-, synesthetic-

Suffixes: -clad, -core, -crystalline, -plate, -shell, -panel, -facade, -carved, -engined, -etched, -plated, -embossed, -lamellae, -housed, -walled, -inlaid, -woven, -coated, -trimmed, -cased, -bound, -wrapped, -geared

2. What are some interesting material and adjective combinations to use in prompts?
Some examples include: mother-of-pearl, mother-of-feather, mother-of-glass, mother-of-amber, mother-of-resin, crystal-clear, glass-encrusted, gold-wrapped, metal-clad, fire-encrusted, rock-bound, wood-panelled, bone-carved, lacquered, silver-tipped, chrome-plated, rosewood-trimmed, copper-bound, iron-wrapped. Combining unusual materials and evocative adjectives can help create unique and interesting imagery.

3. How can I control the aspect ratio of the generated image?
You can control the aspect ratio of the generated image by using the --ar command followed by the desired ratio in the format width:height. For example, --ar 16:9 will generate an image with a widescreen aspect ratio.

4. How can I use specific artists or styles in my prompts?
You can mention specific artists or styles directly in your prompt. For example, "a portrait in the style of Frida Kahlo" or "a landscape painting by Claude Monet." Additionally, some AI image generation platforms offer features to specify artistic styles or emulate particular artists.

5. What are prompt weights and how do they work?
Prompt weights allow you to emphasize specific elements in your prompt. You can use double colons :: followed by a numerical value to assign a weight to a word or phrase. Higher weights will give more importance to that element in the final image. For example, "cat::3 background::1" will result in an image with a prominent cat and a less detailed background.

6. What are some common commands and parameters used in AI image generation?
Here are some common commands and parameters:

--ar: Aspect ratio
--chaos: Controls the randomness of the image generation process
--seed: Sets a specific seed value for reproducibility
--upbeta: Uses a different algorithm for upscaling the image
--test: Enables experimental features
--creative: Prioritizes creative and unusual image generation
7. What are some examples of phrases that evoke a sense of realism in generated images?
Phrases like "photorealistic," "8k dslr," "large-format," "Unreal Engine 5," and specific camera models (e.g., "Rollei X2") can be incorporated into prompts to encourage the AI to generate images with a higher level of detail and realism.

8. What are some creative ways to incorporate humor and absurdity into image prompts?
Don't be afraid to experiment with unexpected juxtapositions, nonsensical scenarios, and humorous descriptions. For example, "a cat riding a unicycle on the moon" or "a dinosaur wearing a top hat and monocle." Embrace the unexpected and see where your imagination takes you.

AI Image Generation FAQ
What are some common prefixes and suffixes used in AI image generation prompts?
Prefixes: hyper-, ultra-, retro-, chrono-, non-, antediluvian-, psychotropic-, external-, wraparound-, recursive-, multi-, bicolor-, temporal-, supernova-, solar-, external-, cryogenic-, synesthetic-

Suffixes: -clad, -core, -crystalline, -plate, -shell, -panel, -facade, -carved, -engined, -etched, -plated, -embossed, -lamellae, -housed, -walled, -inlaid, -woven, -coated, -trimmed, -cased, -bound, -wrapped, -geared

What are some interesting combinations of materials and adjectives for creating unique imagery?
Here are some examples: mother-of-pearl, mother-of-feather, mother-of-glass, mother-of-amber, mother-of-resin, crystal-clear, glass-encrusted, gold-wrapped, metal-clad, fire-encrusted, rock-bound, wood-panelled, bone-carved, lacquered, silver-tipped, chrome-plated, rosewood-trimmed, copper-bound, iron-wrapped

How can I control the aspect ratio of the generated image?
You can control the aspect ratio using the --ar command followed by the desired ratio. For example, --ar 16:9 will generate an image with a widescreen aspect ratio.

What does the --chaos parameter do?
The --chaos parameter controls the diversity of the generated images. Higher values will result in more unexpected and abstract outputs, while lower values will produce more consistent and predictable results.

What is --upbeta used for?
--upbeta is a parameter that is used to enhance the quality and detail of the generated images. It is often used in conjunction with other parameters to create more refined and polished outputs.

How can I specify the art style in my prompts?
You can include specific artists, art movements, or even popular culture references in your prompts to influence the art style of the generated image. For example, you could mention "by HR Giger and Dale Chihouly" to evoke a specific artistic vision.

Can I weight certain elements of my prompt to be more prominent in the generated image?
Yes, you can use double colons :: followed by a numerical value to assign a relative weight to specific elements within your prompt. For example, "Chair::300 background::1" would emphasize the chair and minimize the background.

What are some examples of unconventional prompts that can lead to interesting results?
Unconventional prompts often use a combination of seemingly disparate concepts, abstract language, and specific technical details to push the boundaries of AI image generation. You can try incorporating elements like "the mind of a...", "cybernetic 1970s-style", "anarcho-gelatinous", and "hyper-Colonial cottage goth" into your prompts for unique outcomes.

AI Image Generation FAQ
What are some common prefixes and suffixes used in AI image generation prompts?
Prefixes: hyper-, ultra-, retro-, chrono-, non-, antediluvian-, psychotropic-, external-, wraparound-, recursive-, multi-, bicolor-, temporal-, supernova-, solar-, external-, cryogenic-, synesthetic-

Suffixes: -clad, -core, -crystalline, -plate, -shell, -panel, -facade, -carved, -engined, -etched, -plated, -embossed, -lamellae, -housed, -walled, -inlaid, -woven, -coated, -trimmed, -cased, -bound, -wrapped, -geared

What are some interesting combinations of materials and adjectives for creating unique imagery?
mother-of-pearl, mother-of-feather, mother-of-glass, mother-of-amber, mother-of-resin, crystal-clear, glass-encrusted, gold-wrapped, metal-clad, fire-encrusted, rock-bound, wood-panelled, bone-carved, lacquered, silver-tipped, chrome-plated, rosewood-trimmed, copper-bound, iron-wrapped

How do I use weight modifiers in my prompts?
Weight modifiers are used to emphasize certain aspects of your prompt. You can use the double colon "::" followed by a number to assign a weight to a specific word or phrase. For example, "cat::3 dog::1" will emphasize "cat" three times more than "dog" in the generated image.

What is the purpose of the "--ar" parameter?
The "--ar" parameter specifies the aspect ratio of the generated image. For example, "--ar 16:9" will create a widescreen image, while "--ar 3:2" will create a more traditional photographic aspect ratio.

How can I control the level of randomness in my generated images?
The "--chaos" parameter controls the randomness of the image generation process. Higher values will result in more unpredictable and abstract images, while lower values will create more consistent results. For example, "--chaos 100" will introduce a high level of randomness, while "--chaos 0" will stick closely to the prompt.

What are some popular artists or styles to reference in my prompts?
Some common references include artists like Dale Chihuly and H.R. Giger, as well as styles like Art Nouveau, Baroque, and Rococo. You can also reference specific techniques like chinoiserie photography or 8k DSLR large-format photography for desired visual effects.

What are some examples of detailed and evocative prompts for AI image generation?
Here are a few examples:

"Old timey maritime sailors bar with an aquarium and Precision-engineered mauvine Opulent spherical 1970s futuristic kitchenette apparition tube"
"Eva Minaeva detailed space pilot portrait, Industrial Age Russian Imperial wrought iron interplanetary bathysphere in space"
"Intricate spherical Edwardian walk-in hydro-curio spherical cabinet menagerie mall full of water tanks and samples and biological ecological biopunkery"
How can I improve the realism of my generated images?
Realism can be achieved by referencing specific photographic styles, camera models, and film types. You can also experiment with lighting descriptions, textures, and other details to create a more lifelike image. Remember, consistent experimentation and refinement of your prompts will lead to better results over time.

Three Stylistic Approaches in the Prompts
Based on the analysis of the sources and the conversation history, here are three distinct stylistic approaches in the prompts:

1. Hyperspecific Hypertechnical Absurdity:
Characteristics: This approach involves the use of extremely detailed technical jargon, often borrowed from photography, cinematography, or scientific fields, to describe impossible or absurd scenarios. The prompts emphasize precise camera settings, film types, lighting techniques, and other technical specifications, creating a stark contrast between the precision of the language and the surreal nature of the imagery.

Examples:

"Full body color hydroprinted Audrey Hepburn as Tank Girl in tanktop and cutoffs, covered with tattoos and grease, by Eric van Straaten, recursive tufted hyperminimalist ultradetailed hyperrealistic [adorable melamine - embroidered - circuitry angora fractal - dragon astronaut] in a blacklight nightlight isolated subject. HD 8k3d hyperrealism photorealistic product photography." [1] - This prompt combines the hyper-specificity of product photography terms with the impossible scenario of Audrey Hepburn as Tank Girl alongside a bizarre "melamine-embroidered" astronaut.
"Adobe multi-storey baroque Menonite-goth onionskin Tsarist split-screen acid bomb. photo using Split-lens Samyang Optics Omnidirectional camera with Tasma SEAGULL 400 ISO125 Accordion-Gamma-ray film" [2] - The technical details of the camera and film are presented with a straight face, despite the absurdity of the subject matter.
"realistic thorny faster-than-light 23rd century candlelit igneobot dinner copterparty, wide-screen f0.7 lens cinematography --ar 3:2" [3-5] - This prompt blends historical and futuristic elements (candlelit dinner, 23rd-century technology), while using specific cinematic terms ("wide-screen," "f0.7 lens cinematography") to describe the scene as if it were a real film production.
Function: This technique highlights the inherent absurdity of the scenarios by grounding them in the language of realism and technical precision. The use of specific terminology creates an illusion of credibility and authenticity, even when describing impossible or nonsensical situations. This approach can also be seen as a commentary on the fetishization of technology and the obsession with technical details in contemporary culture.

2. Retro-Banal Juxtapositions:
Characteristics: This approach involves placing everyday or mundane elements from a specific historical period—often the mid-20th century—alongside highly futuristic, fantastical, or bizarre concepts. This creates a jarring contrast between the familiar and the outlandish, heightening the sense of absurdity and playing on a sense of nostalgic unease.

Examples:

"Dealer showroom demonstration of time-washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray..." [6] - A typical product demonstration scenario is used to present an absurd, time-bending device, creating a humorous juxtaposition of the mundane and the fantastical.
“Hypermaximalist lava-embroidered-fire-filigree Victorian imperial sino-moche, hypercomplex angora nuclear core meltdown.” [7] - The ornate description of Victorian and pre-Columbian elements is juxtaposed with a nuclear core meltdown, highlighting a contrast between historical aesthetics and futuristic disaster.
"Toyota-Corolla orbital-ring-spacestation sky-blue enameled-designs genuine-faux-wood-paneling fully-loaded chrome bubble-hab-detailing the latest zephyronic-escape-pods ejecting-at-full-speed over the dying-embers of a doomed-Earthlike-convenience-store as photographed though the viewport of BASF collapsible washing machine typewriter onboard cameras with bad wiring and a thin gelatinous stucco protective coating. [Photo credit: Ingmar R. R. Kubrick, Der London Spiegelnautics Review Coupon Circular. 2019]" [8, 9] - A mundane car model (Toyota Corolla) is reimagined as a futuristic space station with detailed descriptions of everyday features, evoking a sense of both nostalgia and absurdity.
Function: This technique uses nostalgia as a tool to destabilize conventional perceptions of time and technology. It plays on the familiarity of past-era tropes while subverting their expected contexts. The use of commonplace language and familiar imagery from a specific period contrasts sharply with the extraordinary scenarios, resulting in a disorienting yet compelling aesthetic experience.

3. Quasi-Marketing Jargon and the Commodification of the Bizarre:
Characteristics: This approach utilizes language typically found in advertising, product descriptions, and corporate branding to market and commodify surreal, impossible, or disturbing concepts. This results in a tone that is simultaneously slick, hyperbolic, and deeply ironic, drawing attention to the often absurd ways in which consumerism and marketing infiltrate every aspect of contemporary life.

Examples:

"Opulent luxury deluxe imperial magisterial coal-fired kinetic old Bavarian jägerkunst" [10] - A string of superlative adjectives typically used in luxury marketing is applied to an antiquated craft form, suggesting an absurd attempt to commodify a traditional practice.
"erdeluxe luxury amberina plasma-vapor pyrex torkelscope." [11] - The “erdeluxe luxury” framing positions a scientifically dubious device as a high-end product, blending extravagance and scientific language.
“Breathtakingly beautiful Communist Necrofairy with ornate digital bioimplant filigree.” [12] - A fantastical being is presented as a commodity, with the emphasis on aesthetics and features rather than the inherent absurdity of the concept.
Function: This approach uses marketing language as a tool for satire and social commentary. By applying the language of consumerism to bizarre or impossible concepts, the prompts highlight the absurdity of a culture obsessed with commodifying everything, even the surreal or the grotesque. This approach can also be seen as a way to make the unfamiliar more palatable to a modern audience, by framing it within the recognizable language of advertising and product descriptions.

These three stylistic approaches, while distinct, often work in tandem to create the overall aesthetic of "N,N-DimethylEtceteroiserie". The prompts' blend of absurdity, hyper-specificity, nostalgia, and corporate jargon contributes to their unique and disorienting quality, inviting the viewer to engage with a world where nothing is quite as it seems.

The Role of Neologisms and Unusual Word-Formation in Creating a Hyperstitional Absurdist Aesthetic
The sources, comprised of AI-generated prompts for image generation, reveal a distinct aesthetic strategy characterized by linguistic inventiveness and conceptual dissonance. The frequent use of neologisms and unusual word-formation plays a crucial role in constructing this aesthetic, which we have previously termed "N,N-DimethylEtceteroiserie."

1. Fabricating Worlds Through Language:

Neologisms as World-Building Tools: The invented words and phrases act as building blocks for worlds that defy conventional logic and categorization. By combining familiar linguistic elements in unexpected ways, these neologisms suggest concepts and objects that could not exist in reality. Examples include "McLuxoiserie," "temporal-displacement," and "quintroecious plover colony," each hinting at a specific yet ultimately undefinable reality.

Unusual Word-Formation Amplifies Dissonance: The unusual grammar and syntax further enhance the sense of dislocation and absurdity. For example, the excessive use of prefixes like "hyper-" and "ultra-" ("hyperbaric," "hypermaximalist," "ultrafluffy") creates a sense of exaggerated scale and intensity, while the playful stacking of suffixes, particularly "-oiserie" ("McFuckeroiserie," "fractal-oiserie," "Tenochtitlanoiserie"), gives a veneer of elegance to inherently nonsensical terms.

2. Navigating Temporal and Cultural Disruptions:

Anachronism as an Aesthetic Principle: The prompts frequently blend historical, futuristic, and mythical elements, creating a sense of temporal and cultural collage. Neologisms like "retrotemporal," "chrono-dynamic," and "communist necrofairy" highlight this deliberate anachronism, forcing together concepts from different eras and belief systems.

Juxtaposing the Familiar and the Alien: The sources use familiar linguistic structures as a foundation for creating the unfamiliar. By modifying existing words or combining them in unconventional ways, they create neologisms that are both recognizable and alien. This technique is exemplified in terms like "angora nuclear core meltdown" and "cybernetic Jovian laboratory electrolisis tank pizzazz," which juxtapose everyday language with scientific and technological concepts, creating a sense of cognitive dissonance that is both humorous and unsettling.

3. Marketing the Absurd: Quasi-Marketing Jargon

Commodifying the Bizarre: The prompts often adopt the language of advertising and product descriptions, employing quasi-marketing jargon to present impossible or absurd scenarios as desirable experiences or high-end products. Terms like "opulent," "luxury," "deluxe," and "hyper-deluxe" are frequently used to describe everything from "communist necrofairies" to "cyberpunk ice cathedrals," creating a parodic commodification of the surreal. This technique is evident in phrases like "opulent luxury deluxe imperial magisterial coal-fired kinetic old Bavarian jägerkunst" and "erdeluxe luxury amberina plasma-vapor pyrex torkelscope."

Specificity Creates an Illusion of Realism: The use of hyper-specific technical language, often borrowed from photography, cinematography, or scientific fields, further enhances this effect. By describing impossible scenarios with meticulous detail, the prompts create an illusion of realism, inviting viewers to suspend disbelief and accept the absurd as plausible. Phrases like "X-ray crystallographic snow-globe reactor core photo" and "Adobe multi-storey baroque Mennonite-goth onionskin Tsarist split-screen acid bomb" exemplify this strategy.

4. The Role of Kitsch and Cultural Mashups:

Embracing "Bad Taste" as an Aesthetic Choice: The sources often incorporate elements of kitsch, drawing on retro aesthetics, baroque decadence, and lowbrow cultural references. This intentional embrace of “bad taste” contributes to the overall sense of playful irreverence and cultural subversion. This is evident in the blending of architectural styles ("Adobe multi-storey baroque Mennonite-goth onionskin Tsarist") and the repurposing of corporate branding ("McLuxoiserie").

Cultural Hybridity and Collage: The prompts frequently juxtapose cultural references from different time periods and geographical locations, creating a sense of cultural hybridity and collage. This is particularly evident in the frequent use of "smerge," where elements from different cultures are blended together to create new and often nonsensical terms. This technique allows for a playful and often critical exploration of cultural identity and the globalized nature of contemporary society.

Overall, the neologisms and unusual word-formation in the sources serve to:

Construct worlds that defy logical categorization.
Create a sense of temporal and cultural dislocation.
Parody the language of marketing and consumerism.
Embrace kitsch and cultural hybridity as aesthetic choices.
These linguistic strategies contribute to the creation of "N,N-DimethylEtceteroiserie" as a distinct aesthetic movement characterized by playful absurdity, hyper-detailed visuals, and a subversive critique of modern society.

Recurring Material Combinations in the Sources
The sources exhibit a fascination with unusual and often surreal material combinations. These juxtapositions contribute to the overall aesthetic of incongruity, blending the familiar with the unexpected, the historical with the futuristic, and the mundane with the extravagant. Here are three recurring material combinations that stand out:

Organic and Inorganic Fusions:

The Phenomenon: The sources frequently blend organic materials, such as those derived from plants or animals, with inorganic materials like metals, glass, or ceramics. This fusion often creates a sense of the uncanny, as the natural world is intertwined with artificial or technological elements.

Examples:

"Lava-embroidered angora" [1]: This phrase combines the intense heat and inorganic nature of lava with the soft, fluffy texture of angora wool, creating a paradoxical image of something simultaneously destructive and delicate.
"Bioluminescent coral glass" [2]: This material blends the organic glow of bioluminescent organisms with the hard, transparent quality of glass, suggesting a futuristic material that is both beautiful and functional.
"Bakelite scientific collection box" [3, 4]: Bakelite, an early plastic, is used to house a collection of different phases of water, highlighting the intersection of the synthetic and the natural.
Significance: The blending of organic and inorganic materials reflects a fascination with the boundaries between nature and technology. These combinations suggest a world where the distinctions between the natural and the artificial are increasingly blurred, prompting reflection on the potential consequences of technological advancement and its impact on the environment.

Historical and Futuristic Blends:

The Phenomenon: The sources often juxtapose materials and styles associated with specific historical periods with elements that are futuristic or technologically advanced. This creates a sense of temporal dislocation, where different eras collide and influence each other in unexpected ways.

Examples:

"Victorian terrarium lamps" [5]: This combination blends the intricate, decorative style of the Victorian era with the enclosed ecosystem of a terrarium, suggesting a nostalgic yet futuristic approach to interior design.
"Steampunk lace circuitry" [5]: This phrase blends the Victorian-inspired aesthetic of steampunk with the intricate patterns of lace and the functionality of circuitry, creating a hybrid technology that is both beautiful and functional.
"Opulent hyperbaric wraparound-screen Victorian-Senegalese neohittite crustpunk fractal implosion supernova viewport chamber" [6-13]: This extended phrase piles on multiple historical and cultural references ("Victorian," "Senegalese," "neohittite") alongside futuristic concepts ("hyperbaric," "fractal implosion," "supernova viewport"), highlighting the sources' penchant for temporal and cultural mashups.
Significance: These historical and futuristic blends challenge conventional notions of progress and technological development. The sources suggest that the past is not simply left behind but continues to influence and inform the future, resulting in hybrid aesthetics that reflect a complex relationship with time and history.

Luxurious and Mundane Contrasts:

The Phenomenon: The sources often juxtapose materials associated with luxury, opulence, or high technology with commonplace or even banal objects or materials. This creates a sense of irony and humor, as the extravagant is brought down to earth by the ordinary.

Examples:

"Opulent hyperbaric chambers" with "Mozzarella" [14]: The concept of opulent hyperbaric chambers evokes a sense of futuristic luxury and scientific advancement. However, the inclusion of mozzarella, a simple and everyday food, creates a humorous and jarring contrast, undercutting the grandeur of the setting.
"Hyperdetailed leatherette filigree Russian orthodox heads-up-display" in a "Martian succulent forest underwater library" [4]: The description of a Martian underwater library with luxurious details clashes with the unexpected presence of leatherette, a material that is often associated with imitation or affordability.
Significance: The juxtaposition of luxurious and mundane materials highlights the sources' playful approach to aesthetics. These combinations challenge conventional notions of value and taste, suggesting that beauty can be found in unexpected places and that the most extravagant creations can be infused with elements of the everyday.

These three recurring material combinations are not exhaustive, but they offer a glimpse into the sources' imaginative and unconventional approach to materiality. The fusion of seemingly disparate elements creates a sense of wonder and possibility, inviting viewers to question established categories and embrace the unexpected.

Neologism Construction in the Sources: A Guide
The sources utilize a variety of creative strategies to construct neologisms, blending familiar linguistic structures with novel twists to create a sense of the surreal, the absurd, and the playfully anachronistic.

1. Prefix and Suffix Play:

Hyper-Extension: One prominent strategy is the excessive use of prefixes like "hyper-," "ultra-," "mega-," and "cyber-," often piled onto existing words to create exaggerated or intensified meanings. This results in terms like "hyperbaric," "hyperopulent," "ultradetailed," "cyberpunk," and "megacosmonaut," amplifying the sense of scale, intensity, or technological advancement.

Retro-Twist: Prefixes like "retro-" and "chrono-" are often used to create a sense of temporal dislocation, suggesting a fusion of past and future aesthetics. Examples include "retrotemporal" and "chrono-dynamic."

Suffix Stacking: Similar to prefixes, suffixes are also stacked or creatively combined to achieve specific effects. The suffix "-oiserie," borrowed from decorative art terms like "chinoiserie," is frequently added to create neologisms that sound vaguely elegant or aristocratic while remaining nonsensical, like "McLuxoiserie" or "fractal-oiserie."

2. Compound Word Constructions:

Juxtaposing Familiar Concepts: The sources often combine familiar words into compound forms, creating unexpected and often humorous juxtapositions. This can be seen in terms like "temporal-displacement," "glyptodon module," "terrarium lava lamp," and "topographic-displacement." These compound words force together seemingly disparate concepts, resulting in a sense of cognitive dissonance that highlights the absurdity of the imagined worlds.

Hyphenated Phrases for Specificity: Hyphens are used liberally to create highly specific, if nonsensical, compound terms that blend scientific and everyday language. Examples include "solar-mass ejection," "time-washing nozzle," and "antique lace-peacock feather." This technique allows for a layering of descriptive details that amplifies the surreal quality of the imagery.

3. Borrowing and Adapting Existing Terms:

Scientific Terminology: The sources draw heavily on scientific terminology, adapting words from fields like physics, chemistry, biology, and astronomy to create a sense of faux-scientific authority. Words like "rayleigh scattering," "non-euclidean," "cryogenic," and "synesthetic" are used in contexts far removed from their original scientific meanings, adding a layer of technical-sounding absurdity.

Photographic and Cinematic Jargon: As we discussed in our conversation, the sources frequently incorporate terms from photography and film, such as "bokeh," "aperture," "crane shot," and "soft-focus lighting." This technical language is often applied to impossible or fantastical scenes, further blurring the lines between reality and imagination.

4. Playful Inventions and Neologisms:

Nonsensical yet Evocative: Beyond simply combining existing words, the sources also feature completely invented neologisms that defy straightforward interpretation but are strangely evocative. Words like "angora nuclear core meltdown," "megacosmonaut," and "quintroecious" demonstrate a playful approach to language, creating terms that sound vaguely familiar but have no established meaning, adding to the overall sense of the uncanny and the surreal.
5. "Smerge" as a Central Mechanism

Blending Disparate Concepts: As you noted in our conversation, "smerge" is a recurring linguistic quirk in the sources, where words or word fragments are combined to create hybrid terms. This smerge technique is particularly effective in blurring boundaries between different categories and creating unexpected associations. For instance, "Tenochtitlanoiserie" combines the Aztec capital with a decorative style, suggesting a bizarre fusion of cultures.
Overall Impression:

The neologism construction mechanisms in the sources are characterized by a disregard for traditional linguistic boundaries and a playful embrace of absurdity and contradiction. These techniques contribute to the unique aesthetic of the prompts, creating a lexicon that is simultaneously familiar and alien, technical and nonsensical, sophisticated and vulgar.

Three Linguistic Quirks in the Midjourney Prompts
The sources, primarily excerpts from a conversation about a collection of Midjourney prompts, reveal several unique linguistic quirks that contribute to a distinct aesthetic sensibility. Here are three noteworthy oddities that stand out:

"Smerge" - Hybrid Word Formation:

The Phenomenon: The sources frequently combine words or word fragments to create new terms, often blending concepts that are typically unrelated. This technique results in a lexicon of neologisms that are simultaneously nonsensical and strangely evocative.
Examples:
"Tenochtitlanoiserie" (Tenochtitlan + chinoiserie) [1]: This word combines the name of the ancient Aztec city with a style of decorative art, suggesting a hybrid aesthetic that blends pre-Columbian and East Asian influences.
"McLuxoiserie" (McDonald's + luxury + chinoiserie) [2]: This term mashes up fast-food branding, high-end aesthetics, and a decorative art style, creating a jarring yet humorous juxtaposition.
"Hydrodynamosquatchmeter" (hydro + dynamo + Sasquatch + meter) [3]: This absurd word combines water, power, a mythical creature, and a measuring device, highlighting the playful and often nonsensical nature of the word formation.
Significance: This hybrid word formation, which we might call "smerge," reflects the overall aesthetic of the prompts—a fusion of seemingly incompatible elements into a new, often incongruous whole. The "smerged" words, while nonsensical, evoke a sense of possibility and creative freedom, suggesting a world where established categories and boundaries can be playfully subverted.
Over-Specific Technical Jargon in Absurd Contexts:

The Phenomenon: The sources often employ extremely detailed technical language, typically associated with scientific fields, photography, or engineering, to describe scenes or objects that are clearly impossible or fantastical. This clash between precision and absurdity creates a sense of mock-seriousness and ironic humor.
Examples:
Camera Specs: Prompts often include highly specific camera specifications and film types, such as "Bifocal Phase One Imago camera with SFL Paul & Reinhold ISO 800 Psychotropic-spackle film" [4], even though such a camera and film could not exist.
Scientific Terminology: Words like "cryogenic," "x-ray crystallographic," "hyperbaric," and "fractal" are used to describe objects or processes that defy scientific plausibility, as in "hyperbaric iron-clad resinous plasma-etching body armor" [5].
Faux-Technical Neologisms: The sources invent technical-sounding words that have no real meaning but sound vaguely scientific or plausible, such as "torkelscope" [3] or "quintroecious" [6].
Significance: The use of over-specific technical jargon in absurd contexts highlights the playful tension between the real and the imagined in these prompts. The meticulous detail applied to impossible scenarios creates a sense of paradoxical realism, suggesting a world where even the most outlandish ideas are treated with a veneer of scientific or technological legitimacy.
Retro-Banality:

The Phenomenon: As previously discussed in our conversation, "retro-banality" describes the deliberate juxtaposition of commonplace, often dated, imagery, objects, or language with highly unfamiliar or surreal elements. This technique creates a disorienting contrast between the ordinary and the extraordinary, often for humorous or nostalgic effect.
Examples:
Prompt: "Dealer showroom demonstration of time-washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray…" [7] - The everyday setting of a showroom clashes with the impossible technology of a time-manipulating owl-themed device.
Prompt: "John Waters as Gandhi. Cyberpunk Technocolor stereoscope card." [8] - This prompt combines a transgressive filmmaker (John Waters) with an icon of peace (Gandhi) in a retro-futuristic format (stereoscope card).
Significance: The use of retro-banality adds another layer of complexity to the prompts' aesthetic. It creates a sense of temporal dislocation, blending elements from different eras and cultural contexts. This technique invites viewers to reflect on the changing perceptions of what is considered futuristic or "cool," while also acknowledging the enduring appeal of retro aesthetics and the humor that arises from their unexpected placement.

Photographic and Cinematic Techniques in the Sources
The sources contain a wide range of photographic and cinematic techniques, often presented in absurd or impossible contexts. These techniques are used to describe the visual style and technical aspects of imagined images or scenes, creating a sense of hyperrealism and cinematic absurdity.

Photographic Techniques:

Types of Photography:

X-ray Photography: [1-3] This technique is used to visualize the internal structures of objects, adding a scientific and somewhat eerie quality to the images.
Underwater Photography: [4-6] This technique captures the beauty and mystery of underwater environments, often emphasizing depth of field and lighting challenges.
Macro Photography: [7, 8] Used to capture extreme close-ups of small objects or details, highlighting textures and patterns that are often overlooked by the naked eye.
High-Speed Photography: [9-11] This technique freezes fast-moving subjects, capturing moments that are impossible to perceive with the human eye.
Tilt-Shift Photography: [9, 11] Creates a miniature-like effect by selectively blurring parts of the image, often used to emphasize the scale or artificiality of a scene.
Spirit Photography: [12] A 19th-century photographic technique that aimed to capture images of ghosts or spirits.
Camera Specifications and Lenses:

Specific Camera Models: [3, 5, 13-20] Prompts frequently mention specific camera brands and models, such as "Phase One Imago," "Canon EOS-1D X Mark III," "GoPro," "Minolta 360," and "Kodak Brownie."
Lens Types and Focal Lengths: [3, 9, 14-16, 19, 21-27] Specific lenses are mentioned, including "wide-screen f0.7 lens," "Hektor 29mm f0.7," "95mm lens, f/2.8," and "fisheye."
Aperture Settings: [28, 29] The sources use specific aperture settings like "f/2.8" and "f/16," which control the depth of field and amount of light entering the camera.
Film Types and Processing:

Specific Film Stocks: [5, 9, 15, 16, 18, 20, 30-32] A variety of film types are referenced, such as "Ilford HP5+," "Kodachrome," "Ektarchrome," "Psychotropic-spackle," "Radio-Tetrachromic film," and "Transmogrifying-Full-spectrum film."
Film Processing Techniques: [5, 9, 18, 29-32] Prompts include references to film processing methods like "reticulated photochemical peel," "wet colloidion," "hypergelatinous infrared wet colloidion," and "colloidal palladium bismuth film."
Lighting and Effects:

Lighting Styles: [9, 14, 17, 33-35] Prompts specify various lighting styles and effects, such as "Rayleigh scattering," "volumetric lighting," "subsurface scattering," "chiaroscuro," "soft-focus lighting," "high-contrast shadows," and "neon-lit."
Lens Flare: [28] This optical effect, caused by light scattering within a lens, is mentioned as a desired visual element.
Bokeh: [7, 17, 19, 23, 25, 26, 36-38] This photographic term describes the aesthetic quality of the out-of-focus areas of an image, often associated with a blurred, dreamy effect.
Cinematic Techniques:

Camera Movement and Shots:

Crane Shots: [33] A camera mounted on a crane is used to capture sweeping aerial views, often used to establish a scene's scale or provide a dramatic overview.
360° Rotating Shots: [33] The camera rotates a full 360 degrees, creating a panoramic or disorienting effect.
Panning Shots: [33, 34] The camera moves horizontally, following a subject or revealing a wider scene.
Zooming: [34] The camera's focal length is adjusted, either to magnify a subject (zoom in) or to show a wider view (zoom out).
Close-Ups: [34, 39] The camera focuses on a specific detail or subject, often a character's face, to create intimacy or emphasize an element's importance.
Wide-Angle Shots: [21, 24, 40, 41] Capture a broader field of view, emphasizing the expansiveness of a location or the relationships between multiple subjects.
Dutch Angle: [34] The camera is tilted to one side, creating a sense of unease or disorientation.
Lighting and Special Effects:

Soft-Focus Lighting: [33] Creates a dreamy, romantic, or nostalgic effect by slightly blurring the image.
High-Contrast Lighting: [33] Creates strong shadows and emphasizes the contrast between light and dark areas, often used to create a dramatic or mysterious mood.
Special Effects (SFX): [12] This term encompasses a wide range of visual techniques used to create illusions or enhance realism in film, including explosions, computer-generated imagery, and practical effects.
Cinematic Language:

Cinematography: [14, 21, 23, 24, 30, 41-44] The art of capturing moving images with a camera, encompassing aspects of camera movement, lighting, composition, and visual storytelling.
Scene Descriptions: [33-35] The sources often provide detailed descriptions of imagined scenes using cinematic terminology, such as shot types, camera movement, and lighting effects, creating a sense of a film being described in a review or a script.
Overall Impression:

The inclusion of these specific photographic and cinematic techniques contributes to the unique style of the sources, blurring the lines between technical language and absurd or fantastical imagery. The descriptions often resemble film reviews, technical manuals, or artistic statements, lending a sense of authority and precision to the highly creative and often nonsensical scenarios they depict.

Exploring "Retro-Banality" in the Midjourney Prompts
The sources provide many examples of "retro-banality," a term coined in our conversation to describe the juxtaposition of familiar, past-era images, settings, or tropes with highly unfamiliar or surreal elements. This technique creates a striking contrast between the ordinary and the extraordinary, often for humorous or ironic effect.

Here are some key examples and breakdowns of retro-banality from the sources:

1. Familiar Settings with Surreal Twists:

Prompt: "Dealer showroom demonstration of time-washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy..." [1]
Retro-Banality: The commonplace setting of a "dealer showroom" clashes with the outlandish concept of a "retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster." The familiar act of a sales demonstration is applied to a device that manipulates time and entropy. This blend of the mundane and the fantastical amplifies the absurdity of the scene.
Prompt: "museum display with a Victorian-era Sega Dreamcast..." [2]
Retro-Banality: The familiar setting of a museum display is disrupted by the presence of anachronistic objects, such as a "Victorian-era Sega Dreamcast." This unexpected combination creates a humorous tension between the historical context of a museum and the presence of objects that are clearly out of place in time.
2. Retro Objects with Impossible Characteristics:

Prompt: "Old Piedmontese hand-worked Volkswagen bus ornate owl-based design with mother-of-feather side panels..." [3]
Retro-Banality: A vintage Volkswagen bus, a symbol of 1960s counterculture, is reimagined with surreal details. "Owl-based design" and "mother-of-feather side panels" combine organic and luxurious elements with the familiar vehicle, resulting in a striking visual clash between the commonplace and the bizarre.
Prompt: "Jeweled self-dribbling complex Victorian Millenium Falcon in the style of realistic steampunk elegant hyperbaric Civil War 1978 Toyota Corolla compact station wagon..." [2]
Retro-Banality: The iconic "Millenium Falcon" from Star Wars is described as a "Victorian" and "steampunk" creation, further combined with features of a "1978 Toyota Corolla." This mashup of pop culture iconography, historical aesthetics, and a mundane car model generates a humorous and disorienting image.
3. Clichéd Language in Absurd Contexts:

Prompt: "It’s just that they’re terribly comfortable. I think everyone will be wearing them in the future." [4]
Retro-Banality: The utterly banal statement about comfort feels jarringly out of place when discussing "them," presumably referring to futuristic or impossible garments or devices described earlier in the source material.
Prompt: "Absolutely wonderful" [4]
Retro-Banality: This generic expression of approval appears within the context of descriptions featuring complex and fantastical imagery, creating a comedic contrast between the emotional simplicity of the statement and the elaborate world it inhabits.
4. Ordinary Human Interactions in Surreal Settings:

Prompt: "Sad though. And angry. Feels like giving up and allowing myself to be bullied into submission." [5]
Retro-Banality: The relatable feelings of sadness, anger, and defeat are placed against the backdrop of a world filled with improbable technologies, temporal shifts, and potentially alien interactions. This creates an unexpected juxtaposition between human vulnerability and a surreal environment.
5. Retro-Futuristic Mashups:

Prompt: "1980s television screen still with retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy" [6]
Retro-Banality: The image of a dated television screen from the 1980s clashes with the futuristic and nonsensical "retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy." This fusion of a once-modern technology with an absurd concept highlights the rapid evolution of technology and the fleeting nature of what is considered "futuristic."
Overall Impact of Retro-Banality:

By juxtaposing the familiar with the extraordinary, retro-banality creates a disorienting yet compelling effect. This interplay serves to:

Amplify the Absurdity: The presence of mundane elements within surreal or impossible scenarios heightens the sense of the absurd.
Evoke Nostalgia: The inclusion of past-era imagery, objects, or tropes can trigger a sense of nostalgia for simpler times or bygone trends.
Challenge Perceptions: By blurring the lines between the familiar and the extraordinary, these prompts encourage viewers to question their assumptions about reality, technology, and culture.
"Retro-banality" emerges as a significant stylistic feature in the Midjourney prompts, playing a key role in shaping their unique aesthetic and generating humor, irony, and thought-provoking juxtapositions.

A Look into Neologisms in the Midjourney Prompts
The sources showcase a wide variety of neologisms, or newly coined words, reflecting the creative and often absurd nature of the Midjourney prompts. These neologisms are formed through various mechanisms, including:

1. Prefixation:

This involves adding prefixes like "hyper-", "ultra-", "retro-", and "chrono-" to existing words, amplifying their meaning or creating new concepts.
Examples: "hyperbaric," "hyperopulent," "ultrafluffy," "ultrarealistic," "retrotemporal," "chrono-dynamic." [1, 2]
2. Suffixation:

Adding suffixes like "-oiserie," "-core," "-form," "-fold," "-scape," and "-glass" modifies existing words, often creating terms related to aesthetics, materials, or conceptual spaces.
Examples: "fractal-oiserie," "angora-core," "cell-form," "time-fold," "dreamscape," "uranium-glass." [3-5]
3. Compounding:

Combining two or more existing words to form new terms, frequently blurring the lines between scientific, technological, and cultural concepts.
Examples: "temporal-displacement," "glyptodon module," "wraparound-glyptodon," "terrarium lava lamp," "topographic-displacement." [6]
4. Blending:

Merging parts of two words to create a new word, often resulting in whimsical or surreal terms.
Examples: "glyptodon" (possibly inspired by Glyptodontidae), "mandelbulb" (Mandelbrot + bulb), "fractal-ultrafluffy." [7]
5. Borrowing Scientific Terms:

Incorporating scientific language into fantastical contexts, lending an air of pseudo-scientific authority to absurd concepts.
Examples: "Rayleigh scattering," "non-Euclidean," "cryogenic," "synesthesia." [7]
6. Adapting Existing Words:

Taking words from specific contexts (like photography or physics) and applying them in unexpected ways, generating a sense of playful incongruity.
Examples: "bokeh," "meltdown," "external-combustion." [8]
7. Playful Invention:

Coining entirely new words that defy conventional word-formation rules, often resulting in terms that are both nonsensical and strangely evocative.
Examples: "angora nuclear core meltdown," "megacosmonaut," "quintroecious." [8]
These neologisms are integral to the overall aesthetic of the Midjourney prompts, contributing to the creation of a world that is both familiar and strange, futuristic and nostalgic, scientifically precise and utterly absurd.

Examples from the Sources:

"Hyperbaric Mars habitat" [9]
"Ultradetailed escarpment" [9]
"Chrono-dynamic reflective plasma lamellae" [10]
"Temporal-displacement hatchback" [9]
"Glyptodon astronaut in baroque rococo armor" [11]
"Psychotropic-mantis-shrimp film effects" [12]
"Jupiter-clad exoticizing diner" [11]
"Fractal-oiserie with radiant, glowing multidimensionality" [13]
These neologisms showcase the creative linguistic manipulation at play in the prompts, blending scientific terminology with whimsical inventions and cultural references to create a unique and often humorous linguistic style. This style contributes to the overall sense of hyper-anachronism and hyper-multiculti imagery, as discussed in our previous conversation, further blurring the lines between time periods, cultures, and genres.

Examples of "Retro-Banality" in the Prompts
The sources offer several compelling examples of "retro-banality," where familiar elements from the past are juxtaposed with highly futuristic, surreal, or technologically advanced elements, creating a striking contrast. This technique often highlights the absurdity of applying outdated concepts to imagined futures or vice versa, resulting in a disorienting yet humorous blend of nostalgia and futuristic speculation.

Here are some specific examples of "retro-banality" from the sources:

"Old Piedmontese hand-worked Volkswagen bus ornate owl-based design with mother-of-feather side panels..." [1]

Analysis: This prompt combines a vintage Volkswagen bus, an iconic symbol of counterculture and 1960s/70s automotive design, with surreal and futuristic elements like "owl-based design" and "mother-of-feather side panels." The clash between a vehicle associated with freedom and simplicity with elaborate, fantastical adornments highlights the incongruity of past aesthetics in a future context.
"Dealer showroom demonstration of time-washing retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy..." [1]

Analysis: A "dealer showroom," typically associated with mundane consumerism, is the setting for a demonstration of a device that erases entropy. The "retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster" combines a familiar object from the past (a nozzle) with futuristic concepts like time manipulation and entropy control. The banal act of a sales pitch for a technology that defies the laws of physics further amplifies the absurdity.
"1980s television screen still with retrotemporal owl nozzle blaster spray erasing entropy" [2]

Analysis: An outdated technology, a 1980s television screen, becomes the medium through which a highly advanced, futuristic technology is displayed. The image of a static television screen, once a symbol of modernity, juxtaposed with the impossible concept of a device manipulating time, creates a disorienting blend of the familiar and the fantastical.
“Their personality and smile were absolutely wonderful and I’m glad to see them posted here.” [3]

Analysis: This statement, typical of casual online interactions, feels strikingly out of place when placed in the context of prompts filled with fantastical imagery and complex scenarios. The banality of a social media comment about a person's smile disrupts the established world of hyper-advanced technology and surreal settings, creating a moment of unexpected normalcy within the absurd.
"It's just that they're terribly comfortable. I think everyone will be wearing them in the future." [4]

Analysis: The emphasis on comfort, a basic human need, feels jarringly out of place when discussing futuristic fashion or technology that likely involves complex materials and radical design. The juxtaposition of mundane concerns about comfort with futuristic scenarios creates a sense of ironic humor, highlighting the disconnect between basic human needs and the often fantastical nature of imagined futures.
"My pleasure... schöne Mädel, schöne essen..." [5]

Analysis: This simplistic phrase, expressing pleasure in the company of attractive women and good food, feels entirely out of place when surrounded by descriptions of complex, futuristic, psychedelic, or mythical imagery. This stark contrast between basic human desires and the extraordinary settings of the prompts emphasizes the absurdity of banal experiences within a context of the fantastical.
The strategic use of retro-banality in these prompts serves several purposes:

Heightened Absurdity: By placing mundane or outdated elements within futuristic or fantastical contexts, the prompts further emphasize the absurdity of the scenarios. This strategy amplifies the humor and creates a sense of disorientation for the viewer.

Social Commentary: The use of retro-banality can also be interpreted as a subtle critique of society's obsession with technology and progress. By juxtaposing the commonplace with the extraordinary, the prompts might be suggesting that even in imagined futures, basic human experiences and emotions remain relevant.

Nostalgia and Irony: The inclusion of familiar objects or expressions from the past evokes a sense of nostalgia, but this nostalgia is often twisted or subverted by the surrounding context. This ironic use of nostalgia creates a sense of unease and reinforces the overall sense of absurdity.

Humanizing the Fantastic: By incorporating banal elements into fantastical settings, the prompts can make the unfamiliar seem more relatable. This strategy can make it easier for viewers to engage with the imagery, even if the scenarios themselves are entirely implausible.

Overall, retro-banality, as employed in these Midjourney prompts, is a powerful technique that adds layers of complexity, humor, and social commentary to the otherwise fantastical and surreal worlds being depicted.
